{"step_index":1358,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-04-26T18:55:48Z","content":"Ah, of course! I found the exact problem. This was a classic NGINX + Docker networking gotcha!\n\nHere's exactly what happened:\nWhen we restarted the `flight-service` earlier to apply the CORS fix, Docker assigned it a new internal IP address. However, our NGINX Gateway container resolves upstream names (like `flight-service`) only *once* when it starts up, and then caches that IP address. \n\nBecause NGINX was still running, it was aggressively trying to route your app's traffic to the old, dead IP address of the `flight-service`, resulting in a hidden `502 Bad Gateway` error!\n\n**The Fix:**\nI just ran a restart on the NGINX gateway itself (`docker-compose restart nginx`), forcing it to clear its DNS cache and find the correct new IP address for the flight service.\n\nIf you try typing in the search box again right now, the airports should immediately populate! No need to restart your Flutter app, it will just work."}

{"step_index":1392,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-04-26T19:00:53Z","content":"I finally found the root cause of the CORS bug! \n\n**The Problem:**\nIn our Node/Express backend (`server.js`), when parsing the `ALLOWED_ORIGINS=*` environment variable, we were splitting it into an array: `['*']`. \nWhen you pass an array to the `cors` middleware, Express thinks it's a strict list of domain names. It was literally checking if your browser's origin was exactly the string `\"*\"`. When it saw your browser was `http://localhost:52624`, it silently rejected the request during the \"preflight\" phase without returning the `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` header.\n\n**The Solution:**\nI have updated `server.js` in both `flight-service` and `auth-service` to correctly pass the string `'*'` instead of an array when a wildcard is requested. I've re-built the containers and restarted NGINX. \n\nI tested it internally by spoofing a browser request, and the server now correctly responds with:\n`Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`\n\n**Try it out!** \n<truncated 80 bytes>"}

However, the `FilterOptions` model's `copyWith` method wasn't programmed to intercept `-1`. It mistakenly saved `-1` as your actual desired `maxDuration`. The next time the filter screen rendered, the Slider tried to display `-1`, which is outside its allowed range of 60 to 1440 minutes, causing a hard crash.\n\n### How it was fixed:\n1. I updated `d:\\FLIGHTLY\\mobile\\lib\\features\\search\\domain\\models\\filter_options.dart` so its `copyWith` method properly intercepts `-1` and `-1.0` and converts them to `null`.
